TI  - An evaluation of sacituzumab tirumotecan for the treatment of NSCLC.
LID - 10.1080/14712598.2026.2742404 [doi]
AB  - INTRODUCTION: Non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) remains a leading cause of 
      cancer-related mortality, and resistance to targeted therapy and immunotherapy 
      represents a substantial unmet clinical need. Trophoblast cell-surface antigen 2 
      (TROP2)-directed antibody-drug conjugates (ADCs) have emerged as a promising 
      therapeutic class. AREAS COVERED: This drug evaluation summarizes the 
      pharmacological rationale, molecular design, preclinical activity, clinical 
      efficacy, safety profile, and regulatory development of sacituzumab tirumotecan 
      (sac-TMT; MK-2870/SKB264) in NSCLC. Relevant evidence was identified through 
      searches of PubMed, ClinicalTrials.gov, major oncology congress proceedings, and 
      regulatory announcements through 20 September 2026. EXPERT OPINION: Randomized 
      trials have demonstrated improved progression-free survival (PFS) with sac-TMT 
      versus docetaxel and platinum-pemetrexed chemotherapy in epidermal growth factor 
      receptor (EGFR)-mutated advanced NSCLC after EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitor 
      (EGFR-TKI) progression. In advanced NSCLC without actionable driver alterations, 
      sac-TMT plus pembrolizumab improved PFS versus pembrolizumab alone in programmed 
      death-ligand 1 (PD-L1)-positive disease; however, overall survival (OS) remains 
      immature, and superiority over contemporary chemoimmunotherapy has not been 
      established. The principal clinically relevant toxicities are hematologic 
      toxicity and stomatitis. Future studies should clarify predictive biomarkers, 
      optimal treatment sequencing, intracranial activity, and generalizability, while 
      generating mature survival and quality-of-life data.

TI  - Firmonertinib in advanced non-small cell lung cancer with epidermal growth factor 
      receptor P-loop and alphaC-helix compressing mutations.
LID - S1535-6108(26)00430-7 [pii]
LID - 10.1016/j.ccell.2026.09.011 [doi]
AB  - P-loop and alphaC-helix compressing (PACC) mutations comprise a structurally distinct 
      subgroup of epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR) mutations in non-small cell 
      lung cancer (NSCLC) with limited treatment options. Firmonertinib is a once-daily 
      oral, brain-penetrant, mutant-selective EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitor with a 
      unique trifluoroethoxy side chain. Firmonertinib had high anti-proliferative 
      efficacy in EGFR PACC-mutant cells and xenografts. In the phase 1b FURTHER study 
      (NCT05364073), patients with metastatic EGFR PACC-mutant NSCLC randomized to 
      receive first-line firmonertinib 160 mg (n = 25) or 240 mg (n = 22) had confirmed 
      objective response rates (ORRs) of 43% and 68%, median duration of response not 
      reached and 14.6 months, and median progression-free survival of 11.1 and 16.0 
      months, respectively; treatment-related adverse events led to dose reductions 
      (19% and 24%) and discontinuation (3% and 0%). Intracranial ORR among patients 
      with brain metastases at baseline was 47% (8/17). Results support further study 
      of first-line firmonertinib in patients with EGFR PACC-mutant NSCLC.
CI  - Copyright (c) 2026 The Authors. Published by Elsevier Inc. All rights reserved.

TI  - One structural class, one treatment? Firmonertinib in PACC-mutant NSCLC.
LID - S1535-6108(26)00359-4 [pii]
LID - 10.1016/j.ccell.2026.08.003 [doi]
AB  - EGFR PACC-mutant non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) has limited treatment 
      options. In this issue of Cancer Cell, Le et al. report preclinical profiles and 
      promising systemic and intracranial efficacy of the broad-spectrum CNS-penetrant 
      tyrosine kinase inhibitor firmonertinib in PACC-mutant NSCLC, supporting its 
      further clinical development in this challenging mutation class.

PMID- 42887199
OWN - NLM

TI  - A Randomized Phase III Trial of Concurrent Chemoradiotherapy Followed by 
      Durvalumab Consolidation, With or Without Induction Chemo-Immunotherapy, in 
      Patients With Unresectable Locally Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: JCOG2510 
      (PRIME-CRT).
PG  - 39-43
LID - S1525-7304(26)00125-7 [pii]
LID - 10.1016/j.cllc.2026.09.007 [doi]
AB  - BACKGROUND: Definitive concurrent chemoradiotherapy (cCRT) followed by durvalumab 
      consolidation is the current standard of care for unresectable locally advanced 
      non-small-cell lung cancer (LA-NSCLC). However, survival outcomes in this setting 
      remain unsatisfactory. Although the PACIFIC trial improved outcomes, further 
      intensification of systemic therapy may be required to improve long-term 
      survival. Preclinical and perioperative clinical evidence suggests that 
      introducing immunotherapy in the presence of an intact primary tumor may enhance 
      antitumor immune responses. Recently, early-phase studies have reported the 
      feasibility and potential efficacy of induction chemo-immunotherapy followed by 
      cCRT; however, its clinical benefit has not been confirmed in a phase III trial. 
      PATIENTS AND METHODS: This multicenter, randomized, phase III trial is designed 
      to determine whether the addition of induction chemo-immunotherapy to the current 
      standard strategy of cCRT followed by durvalumab consolidation improves survival 
      outcomes in patients with unresectable LA-NSCLC. Previously untreated patients 
      with unresectable LA-NSCLC without activating EGFR mutations are randomized 1:1 
      to receive either cCRT followed by durvalumab consolidation or induction 
      chemo-immunotherapy followed by cCRT and durvalumab consolidation. Randomization 
      is adjusted for age, clinical stage, programmed death-ligand 1 expression, and 
      institution. The primary endpoints are progression-free and overall survival. 
      Secondary endpoints include objective response rate, proportion of patients 
      proceeding to cCRT after induction therapy, and adverse events. A total of 212 
      patients will be enrolled from 60 institutions over 5 years. CONCLUSION: The 
      results of this trial should clarify whether the addition of induction 
      chemo-immunotherapy can enhance curative potential and lead to improved overall 
      survival in patients with unresectable LA-NSCLC.

TI  - KRAS G12S Positive Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Mimicking KRAS G12C Positivity on 
      the AmoyDx PCR Panel: A Case Report.
PG  - e70361
LID - 10.1111/1759-7714.70361 [doi]
AB  - A false-positive KRAS G12C on a multi-PCR assay using the amplification 
      refractory mutation system can potentially occur in cases harboring certain 
      multi-nucleotide variants other than KRAS G12C. A 62-year-old man with a large 
      left upper lobe tumor was diagnosed with lung adenocarcinoma. A multi-PCR assay 
      by the AmoyDx Pan Lung Cancer PCR panel showed positivity for KRAS G12C and KRAS 
      G12A/G12V/G12R/G13C. However, next-generation sequencing revealed a KRAS variant 
      of c.34_35delinsTC (p.G12S). KRAS G12S multi-nucleotide variants may lead to 
      false-positive KRAS G12C results on the multi-PCR assay.
CI  - (c) 2026 The Author(s). Thoracic Cancer published by John Wiley & Sons Australia, 
      Ltd.

PMID- 42883968
OWN - NLM

TI  - Integrated Molecular, Clinicopathological and Immune Profiling of Non-Small Cell 
      Lung Cancer: The MONREPOS Hellenic Cooperative Oncology Group (HeCOG) Study 
      (HE_2TR/13).
LID - 8724 [pii]
LID - 10.3390/ijms27198724 [doi]
AB  - Integrated assessment of tumour genomics and immune biomarkers may improve 
      prognostic stratification in non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC). We examined the 
      distribution and prognostic relevance of somatic pathogenic/likely pathogenic 
      variants (PVs), somatic copy-number alterations (SCNAs), programmed death-ligand 
      1 (PD-L1) expression and tumour-infiltrating lymphocytes (TILs) in a multicentre 
      cohort. This retrospective, multicentre study included 367 unselected patients 
      with histologically confirmed NSCLC treated at 19 centres in Greece between 2000 
      and 2020. Targeted next-generation sequencing (NGS) was performed in 267 tumours; 
      PD-L1 tumour proportion score (TPS) and haematoxylin and eosin (H&E)-based TIL 
      density were evaluated in 298 and 352 tumours, respectively. Overall survival 
      (OS) was assessed using Kaplan-Meier and multivariable Cox regression analyses. 
      TP53 (53.6%) and KRAS (16.5%) were the most frequently detected genes with PVs; 
      SCNAs were identified in 22.5% of sequenced tumours and were more frequent in 
      squamous than non-squamous histology. In multivariable analysis (254 complete 
      cases), TIL density >/=10% (hazard ratio [HR] 0.72, 95% confidence interval [CI] 
      0.52-0.99; p = 0.042) and >/=2 mutated genes (HR 1.44, 95% CI 1.08-1.92; p = 0.014) 
      were independently associated with OS. PIK3CA-containing copy-number gains were 
      significantly enriched in squamous tumours with low TIL density (p = 0.024), 
      while FGFR1-containing copy-number gains showed a similar but non-significant 
      trend (p = 0.088). In the present retrospective study, co-mutation burden and TIL 
      density provided complementary prognostic information beyond clinicopathological 
      variables in NSCLC. The association between selected copy-number gains and an 
      immune-poor squamous phenotype warrants prospective validation.

PMID- 42883213
OWN - NLM

TI  - Small-Molecule Drugs Approved by the FDA in 2025.
LID - 3447 [pii]
LID - 10.3390/molecules31193447 [doi]
AB  - In today's pharmaceutical sector, innovation is not only the core driver of 
      industry upgrading, but also closely tied to patient health and well-being. A 
      drug's innovative mechanism of action, physiological effects, chemical structure, 
      and indications collectively reflect both its clinical therapeutic value and the 
      novelty of underlying basic research. This article presents the innovative 
      advances of 31 small-molecule drugs (accounting for 67.4%) approved by the U.S. 
      Food and Drug Administration (FDA) in 2025. These therapeutics span multiple 
      therapeutic areas, including oncology, autoimmune diseases, infectious diseases, 
      cardiovascular disorders, and metabolic conditions, and target a diverse array of 
      molecular targets such as mitogen-activated protein kinase kinases 1 and 2 
      (MEK1/2), colony-stimulating factor 1 receptor, protein tyrosine kinase Tec, and 
      Bruton tyrosine kinase, among others. Among these approved small-molecule drugs, 
      13 are designated as "first-in-class" therapies, reflecting the continuous 
      innovation in the field of new drug development. Notably, many of these drugs 
      (e.g., Journavx, Brinsupri, Jascayd, and Kygevvi) have also been granted 
      breakthrough therapy designation, which underscores that they not only feature 
      novel mechanisms of action, but also deliver clinically meaningful efficacy 
      advantages over prior therapeutic options. Notably, in 2025, the FDA approved 
      sunvozertinib, a novel small-molecule drug independently developed by Dizal 
      (Jiangsu, China) Pharmaceutical Co., Ltd., for locally advanced or metastatic 
      non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) with epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR) 
      exon 20 insertion mutations, contributing Chinese wisdom and solutions to the 
      transformative evolution of global drug development paradigms. Cross-technology 
      integration, long-acting drug delivery, and precision medicine stand as core 
      industrial trends, enabling innovative drugs to boost therapeutic efficacy, 
      mitigate toxic side effects and refine patients' medical experience, offering 
      patients expanded regimens for improved survival outcomes. With the development 
      of first-in-class drugs, therapies targeting previously undruggable targets-or 
      those inaccessible via conventional approaches-are entering clinical practice, 
      creating major opportunities for novel, mechanism-based drug discovery. This 
      article comprehensively analyzes the mechanisms of action, synthesis methods, and 
      clinical trials of these drugs chronologically, and delineates emerging trends in 
      novel drug development.

TI  - The Dynamic Role of Liquid Biopsy in Patients with Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer 
      Receiving Immune Checkpoint Inhibitors: Emerging Opportunities and Clinical 
      Challenges.
LID - 3240 [pii]
LID - 10.3390/cancers18193240 [doi]
AB  - Immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs) have transformed the treatment of 
      non-small-cell lung cancer (NSCLC), yet predicting durable benefit and detecting 
      resistance remain major challenges. As immunotherapy expands across disease 
      stages, minimally invasive tools are increasingly needed to monitor response and 
      inform treatment personalization. Liquid biopsy enables serial assessment of 
      circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA) and other blood-based biomarkers, complementing 
      tissue analysis and imaging. This review critically synthesizes evidence across 
      resectable, locally advanced, and metastatic NSCLC, integrating ctDNA dynamics 
      with emerging multi-omics approaches. Its contribution is to distinguish the 
      prognostic value of circulating biomarkers from evidence that using them to guide 
      treatment improves patient outcomes, while identifying priorities for clinical 
      translation. Across the reviewed studies, ctDNA clearance is associated with 
      favorable outcomes, whereas persistent or rising ctDNA signals an increased risk 
      of recurrence or progression, sometimes before radiological detection. 
      Methylation, fragmentomics, plasma proteomics, and immune-cell profiling may 
      provide complementary information on tumor-immune interactions. However, low 
      tumor shedding, clonal hematopoiesis, assay variability, and unvalidated 
      intervention thresholds limit routine use. The expansion of perioperative 
      immunotherapy and emerging interest in biomarker-guided treatment adaptation make 
      this synthesis timely. Prospective interventional trials are needed to determine 
      whether liquid biopsy can safely guide treatment escalation, de-escalation, or 
      discontinuation. By clarifying both the promise and the limitations of 
      longitudinal monitoring, this review informs research aimed at detecting 
      ineffective treatment earlier and reducing unnecessary treatment exposure while 
      preserving cancer control.

TI  - AI-Driven Multi-Omics Approaches for Deciphering Small-Cell Lung Cancer 
      Heterogeneity Using Nucleic-Acid-Based Features.
LID - 3159 [pii]
LID - 10.3390/cancers18193159 [doi]
AB  - BACKGROUND: Small-cell lung cancer (SCLC) is a rare but highly aggressive 
      malignancy defined by rapid progression, early metastasis, and profound 
      therapeutic resistance. Its extensive heterogeneity across genomic, 
      transcriptomic, epigenetic, proteomic, and microenvironmental levels continues to 
      undermine precision medicine. Emerging evidence highlights the complex role of 
      epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR) alterations, histologic transformation 
      from EGFR-mutant non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) to SCLC, and the influence of 
      distinct transcriptional subtypes on clinical behavior. METHODS: This review 
      synthesizes current literature encompassing nucleic-acid-based determinants of 
      resistance, SCLC molecular architecture, transformation biology, and subtype 
      evolution, integrating findings from genomic, transcriptomic, epigenetic, 
      proteomic, imaging, and clinical studies. We additionally evaluated artificial 
      intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) methodologies with potential to unify 
      multi-omics, radiomics, and clinical variables to model subtype plasticity, 
      clonal evolution, and therapy-induced rewiring. RESULTS: The evidence 
      demonstrates that traditional single-layer analyses are insufficient to capture 
      the multiscale architecture of SCLC biology or the nonlinear interactions driving 
      resistance, whereas AI-enabled multi-omics approaches offer advantages in 
      enhancing subtype classification, supporting early detection, and improving 
      prediction of therapeutic response. Key barriers such as tissue scarcity, limited 
      biomarker availability, and extreme intratumoral heterogeneity remain significant 
      challenges, though increasingly addressable through integrative computational 
      frameworks. CONCLUSION: AI-driven systems-level integration reframes SCLC as a 
      dynamically evolving, multilayered disease process, where nucleic-acid 
      alterations are central to subtype evolution and treatment resistance, providing 
      a conceptual model to guide biomarker discovery and therapeutic development. By 
      leveraging AI-enabled multi-omics and radiomics, this framework has the potential 
      to advance personalized clinical management and overcome longstanding diagnostic 
      and therapeutic challenges in SCLC.

TI  - Association of Preoperative Circulating Hsp70 Levels with Event-Free Survival in 
      Thoracic Oncology.
LID - 3109 [pii]
LID - 10.3390/cancers18193109 [doi]
AB  - Objectives/Background: Heat shock protein 70 (Hsp70) is frequently overexpressed 
      and released from malignant tumors and is associated with tumor aggressiveness 
      and resistance to treatment. While elevated levels of extracellular Hsp70 
      (eHsp70) in the blood have been associated with unfavorable tumor 
      characteristics, its prognostic significance in the context of long-term 
      follow-up has not yet been sufficiently analyzed. The primary objective of this 
      study was to investigate the prognostic association between preoperative 
      circulating eHsp70 levels and subsequent event-free survival (EFS) in patients 
      with NSCLC undergoing thoracic surgery. Patients with pulmonary metastases from 
      other primary tumors were analyzed as an exploratory subgroup. Methods: The 
      association between eHsp70 levels, clinical outcome, and event-free survival in 
      patients with malignant thoracic diseases was investigated in a retrospective 
      study. Results: A total of 76 patients undergoing thoracic surgery for non-small 
      cell lung cancer (NSCLC) or pulmonary metastases from other primary tumors were 
      included and subsequently followed for disease progression, metastasis, or death. 
      Preoperative eHsp70 levels were significantly higher in patients who experienced 
      an adverse clinical event than in event-free patients (median 194.3 ng/mL [IQR 
      19.9-493.0] vs. 27.8 ng/mL [IQR 14.3-53.4], p = 0.0064). ROC analysis showed 
      moderate discriminatory performance in the overall cohort (AUC 0.68, 95% CI 
      0.55-0.81), with an AUC of 0.72 (95% CI 0.56-0.88) in patients with NSCLC and 
      0.59 (95% CI 0.34-0.83) in patients with pulmonary metastases. In patients with 
      NSCLC, elevated preoperative eHsp70 levels were associated with significantly 
      shorter event-free survival (log-rank p < 0.0001). In multivariable Cox 
      regression analysis restricted to patients with NSCLC, eHsp70 remained 
      independently associated with shorter event-free survival after adjustment for 
      tumor stage, age, and sex (HR 1.33, 95% CI 1.07-1.65, p = 0.010). Conclusions: 
      These findings suggest that circulating eHsp70 may have prognostic relevance in 
      patients with NSCLC. However, prospective validation in larger and independent 
      cohorts is required before its clinical utility can be established.

TI  - Longitudinal Assessment of EGFR and MET Transcriptional Activity Using Cell-Free 
      Chromatin Immunoprecipitation in Two Patients with EGFR-Mutated Non-Small Cell 
      Lung Cancer.
LID - 3097 [pii]
LID - 10.3390/cancers18193097 [doi]
AB  - Background/Objectives:MET amplification represents the most common bypass 
      resistance mechanism to EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI) treatment in 
      EGFR-mutated non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC). Combination therapy with the 
      EGFR-TKI, osimertinib, and the MET-TKI, crizotinib, has demonstrated clinical 
      efficacy in overcoming this resistance. However, assessing MET transcriptional 
      activity during treatment remains challenging. Methods: Cell-free chromatin 
      immunoprecipitation (cfChIP) targeting H3 lysine 36 trimethylation (H3K36me3) was 
      employed to investigate the transcriptional activity of the EGFR and MET genes in 
      serial liquid biopsies obtained from two patients with EGFR-mutated NSCLC. 
      Droplet digital PCR (ddPCR) was used to quantify circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA) 
      and cfChIP enrichment levels. Results: In PT1, H3K36me3 mEGFR and MET cfChIP 
      enrichment increased during disease progression, with MET enrichment increasing 
      further following the initiation of crizotinib/osimertinib combination therapy, 
      during which the patient experienced a partial response. In PT2, MET 
      amplification was detected in a liquid biopsy, but MET cfChIP enrichment remained 
      low throughout the treatment course, and the patient did not respond to 
      crizotinib. Conclusions: This two-patient feasibility study illustrates the 
      potential of cfChIP-ddPCR for the longitudinal assessment of EGFR and MET 
      transcriptional activity in liquid biopsies. These observations warrant further 
      investigation of cfChIP as an approach for monitoring transcriptional activity 
      during osimertinib/crizotinib treatment.

TI  - Biomarker Integration in the Perioperative Management of Early-Stage 
      Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer.
LID - 7644 [pii]
LID - 10.3390/jcm15197644 [doi]
AB  - The concept of personalized medicine has tremendous appeal-tailoring diagnosis 
      and treatment to an individual, rather than a "one-size-fits-all" approach. The 
      management of non-small-cell lung cancer (NSCLC) has rapidly evolved toward 
      strategies increasingly guided by tumor biology and precision oncology. The 
      safety and significant efficacy of biomarker-directed therapies in metastatic 
      disease have fueled interest in applying precision oncology to early-stage, 
      operable NSCLC. Advances in molecular profiling have revealed that a substantial 
      proportion of resected NSCLC harbor actionable driver mutations, enabling the 
      integration of targeted therapies into the perioperative setting. The success of 
      adjuvant epidermal growth factor receptor and anaplastic lymphoma kinase 
      inhibition established a framework for biomarker-driven therapy and highlights 
      the importance of comprehensive molecular testing at diagnosis. In parallel, 
      perioperative immune checkpoint inhibitor-based strategies have expanded 
      treatment options for appropriately selected patients, further emphasizing the 
      importance of molecular profiling. Significant implementation gaps remain, 
      including incomplete molecular testing, delayed results, inadequate tissue 
      sampling, fragmented workflows, and disparities in access. Beyond clinically 
      actionable driver mutations, emerging research highlights radiogenomics and 
      advances in the use of circulating tumor DNA for minimal residual disease 
      detection, with important implications for early recurrence detection and patient 
      prognostication, although these technologies differ in clinical maturity. 
      Collectively, these developments emphasize the molecular heterogeneity underlying 
      early-stage NSCLC and the expanding role of biomarkers in guiding clinical 
      decision-making. Integrating genomic, radiographic, and biologic markers into 
      clinical workflows will be essential to refine risk stratification, personalize 
      perioperative treatment strategies, and ultimately improve long-term oncologic 
      outcomes.

PMID- 42881251
OWN - NLM
STAT- Publisher
LR  - 20261015
IS  - 1476-5594 (Electronic)
IS  - 0950-9232 (Linking)
DP  - 2026 Oct 16
TI  - Dynamic remodeling of the KRAS interactome by direct RAS inhibitors with distinct 
      mechanism of action reveals convergent responses.
LID - 10.1038/s41388-026-04017-y [doi]
AB  - KRAS is mutated in 30% of all human cancers. Small molecule inhibitors targeting 
      KRAS with distinct mechanisms-of-action and variable specificities have entered 
      the clinic, but their full effect on the RAS signaling network remains unclear. 
      Here, we described the impact of RAS inhibition on the KRAS interactome using 
      Proximity-dependent Biotin Identification. Two inhibitors were used: panRAS-ON, 
      which forms a ternary complex between cyclophilin and RAS in the GTP-bound state; 
      panKRAS-off, which binds to the KRAS switch II pocket. RAS inhibitors were found 
      to significantly alter 16.5% of proteins in proximity to KRAS. Despite their 
      distinct modality, the KRAS inhibitors induced highly correlated changes in the 
      KRAS interactomes. Among KRAS-proximal proteins, Afadin was highly affected by 
      RAS inhibition. AFDN is a key regulator of cell motility, invasion, and 
      metastasis. Analysis of AFDN phosphorylation revealed that AKT only partially 
      modulates p-Ser1718, while KRAS inhibition sufficiently abolished EGF-mediated 
      AFDN phosphorylation. Silencing AFDN in a KRAS-driven non-small cell lung cancer 
      model abolished chemotaxis and disrupted directional migration in an EGF-driven 
      wound healing model. We demonstrated that KRAS is a central node in regulating 
      growth factor-induced cell migration, and that KRAS inhibition plays a broader 
      role than MAPK-mediated cell proliferation and survival.
CI  - (c) 2026. The Author(s).

TI  - Serum extracellular vesicle-associated SNORD54 adds diagnostic information to CEA 
      and CYFRA21-1 in non-small cell lung cancer: a case-control development study.
PG  - 102091
LID - S0890-8508(26)00031-9 [pii]
LID - 10.1016/j.mcp.2026.102091 [doi]
AB  - BACKGROUND: Blood-based biomarkers that complement established serum markers may 
      improve molecular classification of non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC). We 
      evaluated whether SNORD54 and SNORD41 measured in a serum extracellular vesicle 
      (EV)-enriched fraction provided incremental discrimination beyond 
      carcinoembryonic antigen (CEA) and cytokeratin 19 fragment 21-1 (CYFRA21-1). 
      METHODS: This single-center case-control biomarker-development study included 
      serum samples from patients with histologically or cytologically confirmed NSCLC 
      and contemporaneous healthy controls. Supporting expression evidence was assessed 
      in paired lung adenocarcinoma and lung squamous cell carcinoma datasets and in 48 
      paired formalin-fixed, paraffin-embedded NSCLC specimens. Serum analyses included 
      gene-specific cohorts and a common complete-case cohort with SNORD54, SNORD41, 
      CEA, and CYFRA21-1 measurements. Logistic regression models were compared using 
      paired DeLong tests. Model parsimony, adjustment for age, sex, and smoking, 
      locked Stage I-II and Stage I subgroup analyses, U6-normalization sensitivity 
      analyses, and 2,000-resample bootstrap internal validation were performed. 
      RESULTS: The complete-case cohort comprised 98 patients with NSCLC and 79 healthy 
      controls. Adding SNORD54 to CEA and CYFRA21-1 increased the AUC from 0.851(95% 
      confidence interval [CI], 0.795-0.907) to 0.951 (95% CI, 0.920-0.982; DeltaAUC, 
      0.100; 95% CI, 0.050-0.150; P<0.001). At the development-derived cutoff, the 
      three-marker model achieved 85.7% sensitivity and 94.9% specificity. SNORD41 did 
      not improve the SNORD54-containing model (DeltaAUC, 0.0003; P=0.480; likelihood-ratio 
      P=0.953) and increased both Akaike and Bayesian information criteria. With 
      coefficients and the cutoff fixed from the overall cohort, the three-marker model 
      achieved AUCs of 0.904 (95% CI, 0.843-0.964) for Stage I-II disease and 0.882 
      (95% CI, 0.804-0.960) for Stage I disease. SNORD54 remained informative after 
      adjustment for age, sex, and smoking. The optimism-corrected AUC was 0.948. U6 Ct 
      values differed between diagnostic groups; associations of both snoRNAs with case 
      status persisted in sensitivity models accounting for U6 Ct. CONCLUSIONS: SNORD54 
      measured in a serum EV-enriched fraction provided incremental discrimination 
      beyond CEA and CYFRA21-1 in this NSCLC case-control cohort. The parsimonious 
      SNORD54 + CEA + CYFRA21-1 model retained discrimination in locked early-stage 
      subgroup analyses and showed limited apparent optimism after bootstrap 
      correction. Independent validation in cohorts that include benign pulmonary 
      nodules and other relevant lung diseases, together with standardized EV 
      processing and validated RNA normalization, will define the clinical 
      transportability of this three-marker panel.

TI  - LAPTM4B in tumor immune evasion: lysosomal autophagy, metabolic adaptation, and 
      therapeutic resistance.
PG  - 1946009
LID - 10.3389/fimmu.2026.1946009 [doi]
LID - 1946009
AB  - BACKGROUND: Tumor immune evasion is shaped not only by canonical immune 
      checkpoints but also by lysosomal stress adaptation, autophagy, metabolic 
      remodeling, and therapy-induced survival programs within malignant cells. 
      Lysosomal-associated protein transmembrane 4 beta (LAPTM4B), an oncogenic 
      lysosomal transmembrane protein, has emerged as a candidate regulator of these 
      interconnected processes through its roles in lysosomal homeostasis, autophagic 
      flux, receptor trafficking, metabolic adaptation, and therapeutic resistance. 
      MAIN BODY: Experimental studies support important roles for LAPTM4B in lysosomal 
      homeostasis, autophagic regulation, and multiple forms of therapy resistance. 
      Additional evidence links autophagy-dependent stress adaptation to HIF-1alpha 
      stabilization, glycolytic remodeling, lactate accumulation, and immunoregulatory 
      changes within the TME. On the basis of these direct and indirect observations, 
      this review proposes a conceptual model in which LAPTM4B may function as a 
      context-dependent organizing node that helps couple lysosomal stress tolerance, 
      metabolic adaptation, and immune escape in selected tumor settings. In this 
      framework, LAPTM4B may contribute to sustained autophagic flux under stress, 
      support metabolic conditions compatible with aerobic glycolysis, and potentially 
      facilitate downstream immunosuppressive processes, including lactate-associated 
      microenvironmental remodeling. We also review structural heterogeneity of 
      LAPTM4B, clinical associations across multiple malignancies, multidrug-resistance 
      mechanisms, and emerging evidence for EGFR-TKI escape mediated by 
      LAPTM4B-dependent stabilization of ATP1A1 in non-small cell lung cancer. 
      CONCLUSIONS: Current evidence supports LAPTM4B as an important regulator of 
      lysosomal adaptation and therapy resistance, whereas its broader role in tumor 
      immune evasion remains a testable model rather than an experimentally settled 
      mechanism. We therefore present the autophagy-metabolism-immunity axis as a 
      layered framework: experimentally supported in selected tumor contexts, 
      indirectly supported by related metabolic and immune observations, and 
      hypothesis-generating where direct causal evidence remains absent.

TI  - CXCL9-driven immune rewiring and tumor-intrinsic ATR signaling inhibition restore 
      radiotherapy response in lung cancer.
LID - 10.1186/s13046-026-03834-z [doi]
LID - 218
AB  - BACKGROUND: Non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) accounts for ~ 85% of lung cancers 
      and is associated with poor outcomes in advanced stages. Radiotherapy (RT) is 
      central to NSCLC treatment, yet locoregional relapse remains frequent. Increasing 
      evidence indicates that, beyond tumor-intrinsic adaptations, the tumor 
      microenvironment (TME) and immune contexture critically influence response to 
      irradiation. However, most radioresistance models rely on human cell lines 
      unsuitable for immunocompetent systems, limiting insight into immune-tumor 
      interactions. METHODS: We generated radioresistant (RR) isogenic variants of 
      murine lung adenocarcinoma cell lines and investigated RT resistance using 
      transcriptomics, 3D co-culture systems, and syngeneic mouse models. RESULTS: RR 
      clones showed stable adaptations to DNA damage, including enhanced clonogenic 
      survival, reduced immunogenic cell death, and constitutive activation of DNA 
      damage response pathways, particularly ATR signaling. High ATR levels in NSCLC 
      patients were significantly associated with immunosuppression. Transcriptomics 
      revealed altered DNA repair and downregulation of type-I interferon and innate 
      immune pathways. In vivo, RR tumors exhibited an immunosuppressive TME with 
      increased T regulatory cells, M2-like macrophages, and myeloid-derived suppressor 
      cells, alongside reduced cytotoxic T cell recruitment. In 3D co-cultures, 
      immunosuppressive cells protected cancer cells from RT-induced cytotoxicity. 
      Notably, RR tumors regained RT sensitivity in immunodeficient mice, highlighting 
      the role of immune suppression in radioresistance. Therapeutically, activation of 
      innate immunity using TLR3-TLR7/8 agonists (poly(I:C)+R848) restored antitumor 
      responses and improved RT efficacy, partly via CXCL9/10-mediated immune 
      recruitment. ATR inhibition with ceralasertib further sensitized RR tumors to RT. 
      CONCLUSION: These findings identify cancer cell-intrinsic ATR signaling and 
      immune suppression as key drivers of radioresistance and support combined 
      therapeutic strategies to prevent locoregional relapse in NSCLC after 
      radiotherapy.
CI  - (c) 2026. The Author(s).

TI  - PiRNA-Hsap_1847317 may serve as a novel biomarker of PD-1 inhibitor resistance in 
      patients with advanced non-small cell lung cancer.
PG  - 103071
LID - S1936-5233(26)00407-9 [pii]
LID - 10.1016/j.tranon.2026.103071 [doi]
AB  - BACKGROUND: Acquired resistance to programmed cell death protein 1 (PD-1) 
      inhibitors presents a major clinical challenge in the treatment of non-small cell 
      lung cancer (NSCLC). This study aims to elucidate the epigenetic mechanisms 
      underlying PD-1 inhibitor resistance, with a specific focus on the expression 
      profiles of PIWI-interacting RNAs (piRNAs) and their associated signaling 
      networks. METHODS: High-throughput RNA sequencing was performed on matched pre- 
      and post-resistance plasma samples from three NSCLC patients to identify 
      differentially expressed piRNAs. The top five candidate piRNAs were selected for 
      subsequent verification. Plasma piRNA levels and comprehensive clinical data from 
      an independent cohort of 50 NSCLC patients (evaluated at both pre- and 
      post-resistance stages) were analyzed. Downstream target genes of the candidate 
      piRNAs were predicted using TargetScan and miRanda algorithms, followed by Gene 
      Ontology (GO) and Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes (KEGG) functional 
      enrichment analyses. Target gene expression was experimentally validated via 
      quantitative real-time PCR (qRT-PCR). Spearman's rank correlation was utilized to 
      assess the relationship between piRNA expression and systemic inflammatory 
      indices. Diagnostic performance was evaluated using receiver operating 
      characteristic (ROC) curves. RESULTS: Five candidate piRNAs (Hsap_2765751, 
      Hsap_249, Hsap_1847317, Hsap_681799, and Hsap_857420) exhibited significant 
      differential expression. Notably, Hsap_1847317 was robustly upregulated following 
      the development of resistance (P < 0.001). Bioinformatic and experimental 
      analyses implicated Hsap_1847317 is implicated in the Wnt signaling pathway, 
      while qRT-PCR demonstrated significantly higher AXIN2 mRNA expression after 
      resistance (P < 0.001). these associative data do not establish direct 
      regulation. Furthermore, the systemic neutrophil-to-lymphocyte ratio (NLR) 
      significantly increased during the resistance phase (P < 0.001) and exhibited a 
      positive correlation with Hsap_1847317 expression (r = 0.395, P = 0.005). ROC 
      curve analysis demonstrated excellent predictive efficacy, yielding an area under 
      the curve (AUC) of 0.944 for Hsap_1847317 and 0.720 for NLR in this cohort. 
      CONCLUSION: Our findings indicate that plasma Hsap_1847317 and NLR are candidate 
      non-invasive biomarkers for monitoring acquired resistance to PD-1 inhibitors in 
      NSCLC. This study association between Hsap_1847317, Wnt-related enrichment, and 
      AXIN2 mRNA provides a hypothesis for further mechanistic testing rather than 
      evidence of a causal Hsap_1847317/Wnt/AXIN2 axis.

TI  - Intracranial activity of amivantamab combined with carboplatin and pemetrexed in 
      epidermal growth factor receptor exon 20 insertion-positive non-small cell lung 
      cancer with brain metastases: a report of three cases.
PG  - 191
LID - 10.21037/acr-2026-0096 [doi]
LID - 191
AB  - BACKGROUND: Epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR) exon 20 insertion (Ex20ins) 
      mutations in non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) are largely insensitive to 
      conventional EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs) because of steric hindrance 
      in the TKI-binding pocket. Amivantamab, an EGFR-MET bispecific antibody, has been 
      approved for use in combination with chemotherapy as the first-line treatment for 
      EGFR Ex20ins NSCLC. However, intracranial activity remains unknown because 
      monoclonal antibodies are generally thought to have limited central nervous 
      system (CNS) penetration. CASE DESCRIPTION: We report three patients with 
      advanced NSCLC with EGFR Ex20ins and with asymptomatic brain metastases who were 
      treated with carboplatin, pemetrexed, and amivantamab (PAPILLON regimen). Case 1 
      was a 48-year-old woman with >/=10 asymptomatic brain metastases that developed 
      during prior treatment with carboplatin, pemetrexed, and an investigational TKI 
      targeting their EGFR ex20ins mutations; she achieved intracranial and 
      extracranial partial responses. Case 2 was a 67-year-old man with postoperative 
      recurrence and new brain metastases arising during another investigational 
      EGFR-TKI monotherapy; he achieved a complete intracranial response. Case 3 was a 
      35-year-old man with previously untreated advanced lung adenocarcinoma and 
      multiple asymptomatic brain metastases, which demonstrated intracranial and 
      extracranial partial responses within two months. CONCLUSIONS: These three cases 
      provide clinical evidence that carboplatin, pemetrexed, and amivantamab may exert 
      clinically meaningful intracranial antitumor activity in patients with EGFR 
      Ex20ins NSCLC harboring small, asymptomatic brain metastases. Amivantamab, a 
      bispecific antibody, combined with carboplatin and pemetrexed may exert 
      clinically meaningful intracranial antitumor activity.
CI  - (c) AME Publishing Company.

TI  - Prolonged survival in a 57-year-old female with RB1-wildtype transformed 
      small-cell lung cancer treated with immunochemotherapy and antiangiogenic 
      combined therapy: a case report.
PG  - 179
LID - 10.21037/acr-2026-0089 [doi]
LID - 179
AB  - BACKGROUND: The transformed small-cell lung cancer (T-SCLC) has a dismal 
      prognosis, yet there remains a lack of standardized therapeutic guidelines. While 
      this transformation is predominantly driven by concurrent loss of TP53 and RB1, 
      retaining wild-type RB1 is exceptionally rare. We report a rare case of 
      RB1-wildtype T-SCLC with prolonged survival to highlight its diagnostic 
      challenges, potential biological mechanisms, and therapeutic management. CASE 
      DESCRIPTION: A 57-year-old non-smoking woman with no significant prior medical 
      history presented with persistent dizziness, headaches, and vomiting. She was 
      diagnosed with EGFR-mutant stage IV lung adenocarcinoma (LADC; EGFR L858R/R776H, 
      PIK3CA E542K, TP53 C176F; RB1 wild-type) following surgical resection of brain 
      metastases. Primary lung biopsy was not feasible due to deep tumor location and 
      marked vascularity. She received sequential dacomitinib [progression-free 
      survival (PFS): 14 months] and osimertinib (PFS: 9 months), monitored by serial 
      imaging and dynamic serum neuron-specific enolase (NSE). Rising NSE prompted 
      re-biopsy of a liver metastasis, confirming small-cell lung cancer (SCLC) 
      transformation at 35 months. She then received serplulimab, anlotinib, etoposide, 
      and carboplatin; significant tumor regression was observed after two cycles, 
      though grade 3-4 hematologic toxicities after the third cycle resolved with 
      supportive care. Liver progression after six cycles prompted second-line 
      nab-paclitaxel plus serplulimab and anlotinib, achieving renewed disease control 
      for over 3 months. Treatment adherence was generally good throughout the clinical 
      course. Overall survival (OS) was 5 years and 4 months, with post-transformation 
      survival of 14 months. CONCLUSIONS: This case highlights a rare RB1-wildtype 
      transformation from EGFR-mutant LADC to SCLC, suggesting a distinct mechanism of 
      lineage plasticity. Dynamic NSE elevation may serve as an early clinical 
      indicator of transformation when tissue re-biopsy is challenging. The durable 
      response achieved with serplulimab-based chemoimmunotherapy combined with 
      anlotinib provides preliminary support for immunotherapy-based strategies in 
      selected patients with T-SCLC.

TI  - Context-dependent value of HLA class I diversity in advanced non-small cell lung 
      cancer treated with immune checkpoint blockade.
PG  - 1924434
LID - 10.3389/fimmu.2026.1924434 [doi]
LID - 1924434
AB  - BACKGROUND: Immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICI), alone or in combination with 
      chemotherapy, are the standard first-line treatment for advanced non-small cell 
      lung cancer (NSCLC) without actionable oncogenic drivers. Programmed death-ligand 
      1 (PD-L1) expression and tumor mutational burden (TMB) are routinely used 
      biomarkers, yet both show limited predictive accuracy at the individual level, 
      particularly in the context of chemo-immunotherapy. HLA class I diversity has 
      been proposed as a biomarker of response to ICI monotherapy, although its role in 
      first-line chemo-immunotherapy remains unclear. METHODS: We performed a 
      retrospective validation and exploratory study including patients with stage IV 
      NSCLC treated with ICI monotherapy or platinum-based chemo-immunotherapy in the 
      first-line setting. Tumors were profiled using a single clinically-validated 
      targeted sequencing assay (TruSight Oncology 500), allowing simultaneous 
      assessment of TMB and HLA class I diversity. Furthermore, tumors had a complete 
      clinical annotation, including the level of PD-L1 expression. Associations with 
      objective response rate (ORR) and progression-free survival (PFS) were evaluated. 
      RESULTS: PD-L1 expression and TMB showed limited discriminative performance for 
      ORR, particularly in chemo-immunotherapy-treated patients. Composite HLA class I 
      diversity did not predict ORR or PFS. A triple-negative biomarker profile 
      identified a subgroup with reduced likelihood of response, although statistical 
      significance was not reached. Exploratory analyses suggested that locus-specific 
      HLA-B diversity, when combined with TMB, was associated with numerically longer 
      PFS, whereas HLA-A, HLA-C, and composite HLA metrics showed no consistent 
      association. CONCLUSIONS: In advanced NSCLC treated predominantly with first line 
      chemo-immunotherapy, composite HLA class I diversity does not improve outcome 
      prediction beyond established biomarkers. Locus-specific HLA metrics, 
      particularly HLA-B, may warrant further investigation.
CI  - Copyright (c) 2026 Ramos-Paradas, Carrizo, Alvarez, Martin-Arriscado, Lopez, Ortiz, 
      Herrera, Rey, Baena, Zugazagoitia, Paz-Ares and Ucero.

TI  - Dacomitinib With or Without Dose Titration in Treatment-Naive Advanced 
      EGFR-Mutated NSCLC - Primary Analysis of the ATORG-003 Phase 2 Trial.
PG  - 101027
LID - 10.1016/j.jtocrr.2026.101027 [doi]
LID - 101027
AB  - INTRODUCTION: Dacomitinib is an approved second-generation EGFR tyrosine kinase 
      inhibitor. In the phase 3 ARCHER 1050 trial, both progression-free survival (PFS) 
      and overall survival (OS) were improved with first-line dacomitinib compared with 
      gefitinib. Post hoc analysis revealed that efficacy (PFS, OS) was similar in both 
      dose-reduced patients and the overall study population. ATORG-003 evaluated a 
      dose-titration strategy to improve tolerability while maintaining efficacy. This 
      is also the first prospective study of dacomitinib's intracranial efficacy and 
      resistance (tissue and plasma next-generation sequencing). METHODS: ATORG-003 
      (Asian Thoracic Oncology Research Group; five Asian countries) was a single-arm, 
      investigator-initiated phase 2 trial that enrolled patients with treatment-naive 
      stages IIIB to IV EGFR-mutated NSCLC. Patients received dacomitinib 30 mg daily 
      for 4 weeks, with a shared investigator and patient decision for titration to 45 
      mg in patients with grade less than or equal to 1 toxicity (Common Terminology 
      Criteria for Adverse Events version 5.0). The primary end point was the 12-month 
      PFS rate (in patients without central nervous system metastases only) compared 
      with the historical control. Secondary end points were time to treatment failure 
      (TTF), objective response rate (ORR), OS, intracranial ORR (iORR) and 
      intracranial PFS (iPFS), and safety and tolerability. RESULTS: From September 
      2019 to October 2022, 118 patients were enrolled. The median age was 65 (range 
      33-84) years, and 54% were female. EGFR mutation type was exon 19 deletion in 
      57%, L858R mutation in 42%, and both in 1%. There were 18% of patients who were 
      dose titrated to 45 mg at cycle 2, and 70% tolerated 30 mg (67%) or 45 mg (3%) 
      without dose reduction. The primary end point was met, with a 12-month PFS 
      proportion rate of 59.6% (90% confidence interval [CI]: 50.6%-68.1%) and a 
      12-month PFS rate of 61.2% (95% CI: 50.5%-70.3%) in the primary analysis cohort. 
      The median PFS was 16.1 months (95% CI: 12.1-19.2), and the median TTF was 19.3 
      months (95% CI: 16.1-22.1). In the overall study population, the ORR was 77% and 
      the median OS was not reached. In patients with central nervous system metastases 
      (n = 24), the iORR was 54% and the median iPFS was 17.6 months (95% CI: 
      7.2-23.1). All patients had treatment-related adverse events (TRAEs), with grade 
      3/4 TRAEs occurring in 25%. The most common grade 3/4 TRAEs were rash (7%), 
      paronychia (4%), and hypokalemia (3%). EGFR T790M resistance was detected in 29 
      of 96 patients (30%) through plasma next-generation sequencing and in 15 of 29 
      patients (52%) through tissue next-generation sequencing. CONCLUSION: ATORG-003 
      prospectively revealed that dacomitinib with dose titration is a viable strategy 
      in advanced EGFR-mutated NSCLC, maintaining efficacy while improving 
      tolerability. Dacomitinib has substantial intracranial activity and high rates of 
      T790M resistance, making it suitable for sequential treatment approaches.
CI  - (c) 2026 The Authors.
FAU - Tan, Aaron C
AU  - Tan AC
AD  - Division of Medical Oncology, National Cancer Centre Singapore, Singapore, 
      Singapore.

TI  - Synergistic effects and molecular mechanisms of pirfenidone combined with 
      EGFR-TKIs in non-small cell lung cancer.
PG  - 272
LID - 10.21037/tlcr-2026-0900 [doi]
LID - 272
AB  - BACKGROUND: Non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) is a leading cause of 
      cancer-related mortality globally. While epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR) 
      tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs), such as osimertinib (OS) and afatinib (AF), 
      have markedly improved clinical outcomes, their efficacy is significantly 
      hindered by the development of acquired resistance. Emerging evidence suggests 
      that p38gamma is involved in the progression of NSCLC and the emergence of resistance 
      to EGFR-TKIs. However, whether the p38gamma inhibitor pirfenidone (PFD) can enhance 
      the sensitivity of NSCLC to EGFR-TKIs remains unclear. This study aims to 
      evaluate the synergistic antitumor effects of PFD in combination with OS or AF 
      and to elucidate the underlying molecular mechanisms. METHODS: EGFR-mutant NSCLC 
      cell lines (PC9 and HCC827) and their OS-resistant derivatives (HCC827 OR) were 
      utilized to evaluate cell proliferation by Cell Counting Kit-8 (CCK-8) assays and 
      colony formation experiments. Western blotting was performed to analyze the 
      expression of p38gamma, proteins within the phosphatidylinositol 3-kinase/protein 
      kinase B/mammalian target of rapamycin (PI3K/AKT/mTOR) signaling pathway, and 
      autophagy-related markers. Drug interactions were assessed using the 
      SynergyFinder platform. Autophagic flux was investigated via a dual-fluorescent 
      adenovirus (mRFP-GFP-LC3) and further validated using the autophagy inhibitor 
      bafilomycin A1. Additionally, an HCC827 nude mouse xenograft model was employed 
      for in vivo validation. RESULTS: The combination of PFD with either OS or AF 
      exhibited significant synergistic antitumor effects in both in vitro and in vivo 
      NSCLC models, consistently achieving Bliss synergy scores exceeding 14. Notably, 
      this combination successfully restored drug sensitivity in OS-resistant cells and 
      reversed acquired resistance to EGFR-TKIs. Mechanistic investigations revealed 
      that PFD enhanced EGFR-TKI sensitivity via two distinct pathways: firstly, by 
      specifically inhibiting p38gamma, a critical mediator of resistance, which leads to 
      the downregulation of the PI3K/AKT/mTOR signaling pathway; and secondly, by 
      promoting the autophagy cascade in a p38gamma-independent manner. These synergistic 
      effects were not reversed by ferroptosis or pyroptosis inhibitors and were only 
      partially attributed to apoptosis. CONCLUSIONS: PFD enhances the efficacy of 
      EGFR-TKIs through a dual mechanism: first, by specifically inhibiting the 
      p38gamma-mediated PI3K/AKT/mTOR signaling pathway, and second, by activating a 
      p38gamma-independent autophagy pathway. The activation of the p38gamma-mediated 
      PI3K/AKT/mTOR pathway may represent a key mechanism underlying OS resistance. 
      This study provides the first evidence that PFD can restore drug sensitivity in 
      OS-resistant cells, offering valuable experimental support for its potential 
      clinical application as an EGFR-TKI sensitizer and a therapeutic strategy to 
      overcome resistance in targeted cancer therapies.
CI  - (c) AME Publishing Company.

TI  - Retrospective real-world associations between EGFR alterations and furmonertinib 
      outcomes in advanced non-small cell lung cancer.
PG  - 287
LID - 10.21037/tlcr-2026-1103 [doi]
LID - 287
AB  - BACKGROUND: Furmonertinib, a third-generation epidermal growth factor 
      receptor-tyrosine kinase inhibitor (EGFR-TKI), has shown promising efficacy in 
      EGFR-mutant non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC). This real-world study aimed to 
      explore efficacy differences among EGFR mutation subtypes and the influence of 
      concomitant genetic alterations on furmonertinib. METHODS: This retrospective 
      multicenter real-world study enrolled patients with unresectable stage IIIB/C-IV 
      EGFR-mutant NSCLC who received furmonertinib (80 milligrams per day). Main 
      eligibility criteria included advanced EGFR-mutant NSCLC, measurable lesions, and 
      available next-generation sequencing (NGS) molecular data. Patients were 
      recruited from five Chinese medical centers. Progression-free survival (PFS) was 
      defined as time from furmonertinib initiation to disease progression or any-cause 
      death; overall survival (OS) was defined as time from furmonertinib initiation to 
      any-cause death or last follow-up. Baseline clinical and NGS-detected molecular 
      data were collected. Survival endpoints included objective response rate (ORR), 
      disease control rates (DCRs), PFS and OS. Kaplan-Meier and Cox 
      proportional-hazards models were applied to evaluate prognostic associations, 
      reporting hazard ratios (HR) with 95% confidence intervals (CIs) alongside exact 
      P values. RESULTS: A total of 139 patients were included, among whom 64 harbored 
      EGFR exon 19 deletion and 74 harbored L858R mutation. TP53 mutations were 
      detected in 64.4% (58/90) of the tested patients. There were 136 patients 
      profiled for additional concurrent driver alterations. Among 131 evaluable 
      patients, ORR was 69.5% and the DCR was 97.7%. Median PFS of the entire cohort 
      was 623 days (approximately 20.8 months); median OS was not reached. Compared 
      with L858R, EGFR exon19 deletion was associated with longer PFS (adjusted HR 
      =1.77, 95% CI: 1.09-2.87, P=0.02). After adjusting for age, disease stage, and 
      EGFR mutation subtype, multiple gene mutations were associated with poorer PFS 
      (HR =4.03, 95% CI: 1.98-8.19, P<0.001) and OS (HR =3.25, 95% CI: 1.07-9.86, 
      P=0.04). No significant prognostic association was observed for TP53 co-mutation. 
      CONCLUSIONS: This retrospective real-world cohort suggests that EGFR subtypes and 
      multiple concurrent genomic alterations are associated with survival outcomes 
      under furmonertinib treatment.
CI  - (c) AME Publishing Company.

TI  - Bioelectrical impedance-derived body composition and systemic therapy outcomes in 
      non-small cell lung cancer.
PG  - 282
LID - 10.21037/tlcr-2026-0718 [doi]
LID - 282
AB  - BACKGROUND: Body mass index (BMI) cannot distinguish skeletal muscle from adipose 
      tissue or capture fat distribution in non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC). We 
      explored whether bioelectrical impedance analysis (BIA)-assessed body composition 
      was associated with systemic therapy outcomes. METHODS: Consecutive adults with 
      confirmed NSCLC who underwent pretreatment multifrequency BIA between March 2023 
      and March 2025 were retrospectively included. Analyses were performed by 
      treatment context, including stage I surgical patients and stage II-IV patients 
      receiving immune checkpoint inhibitor (ICI)- or epidermal growth factor receptor 
      tyrosine kinase inhibitor (EGFR-TKI)-based systemic therapy. Body-composition 
      parameters were compared by clinical stage and EGFR mutation status using 
      analysis of covariance (ANCOVA). Progression-free survival (PFS) was analyzed 
      using Kaplan-Meier methods and Cox proportional hazards models. Objective 
      response rate (ORR) was evaluated using logistic regression. Joint analyses were 
      performed according to skeletal muscle index (SMI) and visceral fat index (VFI), 
      and longitudinal body-composition changes during immunotherapy were also 
      assessed. RESULTS: Among 473 screened patients, 369 were included, comprising 126 
      with stage I disease and 243 with stage II-IV disease. After predefined 
      exclusions, outcome cohorts included 125 ICI-treated and 43 EGFR-TKI-treated 
      patients. In cross-sectional analyses, adiposity-related parameters were 
      nominally lower in stage II-IV disease than in stage I disease, but these 
      differences did not remain significant after false discovery rate (FDR) 
      correction; no body-composition parameter differed significantly by EGFR mutation 
      status. Among 94 men included in the primary ICI PFS analysis, high SMI was 
      associated with longer PFS [adjusted hazard ratio (HR), 0.49; 95% confidence 
      interval (CI): 0.25-0.97], whereas high VFI was associated with shorter PFS 
      (adjusted HR, 2.11; 95% CI: 1.09-4.07). High SMI was also associated with higher 
      ORR among these men [adjusted odds ratio (OR), 3.04; 95% CI: 1.20-7.72]. The 
      combined SMI/VFI phenotype further stratified PFS, with low SMI/high VFI 
      identifying the least favorable subgroup. In an exploratory longitudinal 
      analysis, a >10% decrease in trunk fat mass was associated with shorter PFS. No 
      clear associations between body-composition parameters and PFS were observed in 
      the small EGFR-TKI cohort. CONCLUSIONS: BIA-assessed skeletal muscle reserve and 
      visceral adiposity were associated with outcomes during ICI-based therapy in men 
      with NSCLC. Higher SMI and lower VFI identified a more favorable clinical 
      profile. Prospective validation is warranted to define the role of BIA-based 
      body-composition assessment in host-related risk stratification during systemic 
      therapy.

TI  - Efficacy and safety of osimertinib combined with anlotinib in EGFR-mutant 
      non-squamous non-small cell lung cancer with gradual progression after first-line 
      osimertinib therapy: a single-center retrospective cohort study.
PG  - 285
LID - 10.21037/tlcr-2026-0785 [doi]
LID - 285
AB  - BACKGROUND: Osimertinib, a third-generation epidermal growth factor receptor 
      (EGFR) tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI), has substantially improved clinical 
      outcomes in patients with EGFR-mutant non-squamous non-small cell lung cancer 
      (NSCLC). Nevertheless, acquired resistance inevitably develops, leading to 
      disease progression in the majority of patients. Preclinical and clinical 
      evidence suggests that anlotinib, a multi-targeted anti-angiogenic TKI, may 
      overcome osimertinib resistance. This study aimed to evaluate the efficacy and 
      safety of continued osimertinib in combination with anlotinib in patients with 
      EGFR-mutant non-squamous NSCLC who developed gradual progression following 
      first-line osimertinib therapy. METHODS: This retrospective study enrolled 
      patients with unresectable stage III or IV EGFR-mutant non-squamous NSCLC who 
      received first-line osimertinib therapy at The First Affiliated Hospital of 
      University of Science and Technology of China between January 2021 and December 
      2024. Eligible patients developed gradual progression after first-line 
      osimertinib therapy. Subsequently, patients received combination therapy with 
      osimertinib (80 mg orally once daily) plus anlotinib (10 mg orally on days 1-14 
      of every 3-week cycle). The primary endpoint was progression-free survival (PFS). 
      Secondary endpoints included PFS rates at 6 and 12 months, objective response 
      rate (ORR), disease control rate (DCR), overall survival (OS), OS rates at 12 and 
      24 months, and safety profile. RESULTS: A total of 52 patients were enrolled. The 
      median age was 66 years. 40(76.9%) had an Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group 
      performance status (ECOG PS) of 0-1, 16 (30.8%) presented with brain metastases, 
      23 (44.2%) harbored exon 19 deletions, and 29 (55.8%) had L858R mutations. 
      Molecular testing was performed in 12 patients after gradual progression. As of 
      September 6, 2025, the median follow-up was 34.8 months, 48 PFS events were 
      recorded and 22 patients died. The ORR was 7.7% (4/52), and the DCR was 86.5% 
      (45/52). The median PFS (mPFS) was 9.9 months [95% confidence interval (CI): 
      4.2-12.1], with PFS rates of 61.5% at 6 months and 40.4% at 12 months. The median 
      OS was not reached. OS rates were 86.3% at 12 months and 62.4% at 24 months. The 
      exploratory multivariate COX analysis revealed that ECOG PS [hazard ratio (HR): 
      3.115, 95% CI: 1.111-7.706, P<0.001] and brain metastasis (HR: 2.242, 95% CI: 
      1.341-7.842, P=0.009) were associated with mPFS. Grade >/=3 treatment-related 
      adverse events (TRAEs) occurred in 9.6% (5/52) of patients. The most frequently 
      observed AEs were diarrhea (42.3%, 22/52), hypertension (34.6%, 18/52), 
      proteinuria (30.8%, 16/52), decreased appetite (28.8%, 15/52), and weight loss 
      (28.8%, 15/52). CONCLUSIONS: In this retrospective cohort focusing on the 
      specific pattern of gradual progression following first-line osimertinib therapy, 
      continued osimertinib combined with anlotinib demonstrated encouraging antitumor 
      activity, which warrants further evaluation in prospective comparative studies.
CI  - (c) AME Publishing Company.

TI  - Activity and specificity of KRAS variants-directed Revolution Medicines Company 
      (RMC) compounds for pleural effusion-derived non-small cell lung cancer cell 
      lines.
PG  - 275
LID - 10.21037/tlcr-2026-0798 [doi]
LID - 275
AB  - BACKGROUND: The clinical therapy for KRAS G12C mutated lung cancers has been 
      revolutionized by the approval of the covalent inhibitors Sotorasib and 
      Adagrasib. However, the restriction to KRAS G12C, the response rates of 
      approximately 45% and the duration of response (DOR) of approximately 1 year 
      leaves room for improvements. A completely new approach uses intracellular glues 
      to block KRAS variants by steric hindrance through binding of cyclophilin A 
      (CYPA) to the switch regions of KRAS. A series of Revolution Medicines Company 
      (RMC) compounds were developed, targeting all KRAS variants and KRAS wildtype, 
      namely (RMC-6236, daraxonrasib), KRAS G12C (RMC-6291, elironrasib), KRAS G12D 
      (RMC-9805, zoldonrasib) and KRAS G12V (RMC-5217) with more agents under 
      development. This study aimed to reveal the specificity of RMC compounds and to 
      discover underlying resistance mechanisms in real-world tumor cell lines. 
      METHODS: Four RMC compounds were tested against a panel of 22 tumor cell lines, 
      including 15 non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) lines carrying a range of KRAS 
      mutations that were recently established from pleural effusions of lung cancer 
      patients. Compounds were used in cytotoxicity tests and Western blot arrays 
      comprising oncology-related proteins as well as for KRAS Western blots. RESULTS: 
      The RMC compounds were highly active against the wild-type KRAS cell lines but 
      showed limited activity against the KRAS variant cell lines with approximately 
      50% of the NSCLC lines surviving even at the highest concentration of 2 microM with. 
      The distinct RMC compounds targeting KRAS G12C, G12D and G12V exhibited poor 
      selectivity for the respective NSCLC cell lines, although phosphoproteome 
      analysis showed a uniform downregulation of the phosphorylation of ERK1/2. Cells 
      exposed to RMC-6236 exhibited increased resistance to this drug and Western blots 
      of KRAS revealed either increased protein expression or a shift of KRAS likely 
      attributed to monoubiquitylation that was reported to increase KRAS activity. 
      CONCLUSIONS: The RMC compounds directed at KRAS G12C, D and V mutations exhibit 
      cytotoxic activity against the correspondingly mutated newly established NSCLC 
      cell lines und suppress the MAPK pathway. However, a significant fraction of the 
      cancer cells survives at high drug concentrations and in most cases, the 
      compounds show a poor selectivity for the targeted variant cell lines although 
      they partially contain specific warheads. Increased resistance to RMC-6236 has 
      been associated with elevated expression or likely monoubiquitylation of KRAS. 
      The limited activity of the RMC KRAS(ON) compounds may be supplemented by 
      conventional KRAS(OFF) compounds or other glues or degraders.
CI  - (c) AME Publishing Company.

TI  - Efficacy and safety of third-generation EGFR-TKI rechallenge therapy in advanced 
      non-small cell lung cancer patients with EGFR mutation: a retrospective cohort 
      study.
PG  - 278
LID - 10.21037/tlcr-2026-0596 [doi]
LID - 278
AB  - BACKGROUND: Third-generation epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR) tyrosine 
      kinase inhibitors (TKIs) have become the standard treatment for EGFR-mutant 
      non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC), but acquired resistance remains inevitable. 
      Current post-progression salvage regimens offer only modest clinical benefits, 
      leaving an unmet need for effective late-line treatment strategies. This study 
      evaluated the efficacy and safety of third-generation EGFR-TKI rechallenge as a 
      potential salvage option for heavily pretreated resistant NSCLC patients. 
      METHODS: We retrospectively analyzed 29 patients with advanced EGFR-mutant NSCLC 
      who received third-generation EGFR-TKI rechallenge after progression on prior 
      third-generation EGFR-TKI therapy between December 2019 and August 2025. All 
      patients had pathologically confirmed EGFR-mutant lung adenocarcinoma and 
      received multi-line subsequent treatment after third-generation EGFR-TKIs 
      resistance. Baseline clinical data were extracted from electronic medical 
      records, and follow-up chest CT evaluations were conducted every 6 to 8 weeks. 
      Outcomes were standardized using RECIST version 1.1 for tumor responses and CTCAE 
      version 5.0 for adverse events. Survival outcomes [progression-free survival 
      (PFS), overall survival (OS)] were analyzed via Kaplan-Meier curves and log-rank 
      tests. The primary endpoint was PFS. Secondary endpoints included OS and disease 
      control rate (DCR). RESULTS: The median number of prior systemic treatment lines 
      was 5 (range, 3-12). Third-generation EGFR-TKI rechallenge achieved a median 
      progression-free survival (mPFS) of 6.0 months, a median overall survival (mOS) 
      of 12.0 months. The DCR was 6/29 (20.7%). Treatment-related adverse events 
      (TRAEs) occurred in 8/29 (27.6%) patients, with severe (grade >/=3) events in 3/29 
      (10.3%). Poorer baseline ECOG PS (3-4) was significantly associated with inferior 
      PFS (median: 5.0 vs. 7.0 months, P=0.04) and OS (median: 10.0 vs. 25.0 months, 
      P=0.005). Chemotherapy-containing interval therapy was associated with improved 
      PFS (7.5 vs. 2.0 months, P=0.02). CONCLUSIONS: Third-generation EGFR-TKI 
      rechallenge may provide a feasible and well-tolerated salvage option for selected 
      heavily pretreated patients with EGFR-mutant NSCLC. ECOG PS and prior treatment 
      patterns may assist in patient selection for rechallenge therapy. However, these 
      findings are strictly exploratory and require validation in larger, controlled 
      prospective studies.
CI  - (c) AME Publishing Company.

TI  - Amivantamab plus chemotherapy vs. chemotherapy in participants with EGFR-mutant 
      advanced non-small cell lung cancer after progression on osimertinib: a 
      MARIPOSA-2 analysis of the Asian subgroup.
PG  - 284
LID - 10.21037/tlcr-2026-0202 [doi]
LID - 284
AB  - BACKGROUND: The Phase 3 MARIPOSA-2 study showed that amivantamab, a bispecific 
      antibody with immune cell-directing activity, plus chemotherapy significantly 
      improved progression-free survival (PFS) compared with chemotherapy alone in 
      participants with common epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR)-mutated 
      non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC), whose disease had progressed on osimertinib 
      monotherapy. We conducted a post hoc analysis to assess clinical outcomes in the 
      Asian subgroup of participants from MARIPOSA-2 (NCT04988295). METHODS: 
      Participants were randomised 2:2:1 to amivantamab-lazertinib-chemotherapy, 
      chemotherapy alone, or amivantamab-chemotherapy. The primary endpoint was PFS by 
      blinded independent central review (BICR) per Response Evaluation Criteria in 
      Solid Tumors v1.1 (RECIST 1.1). Secondary endpoints included objective response 
      rate (ORR), intracranial PFS, and safety. Due to mid-study modifications to the 
      amivantamab-lazertinib-chemotherapy dosing schedule, follow-up of this group is 
      ongoing. We present results for the amivantamab-chemotherapy vs. chemotherapy 
      arms in the Asian subgroup. RESULTS: There were 190 participants in the Asian 
      subgroup, 63 in the amivantamab-chemotherapy group and 127 in the chemotherapy 
      group. At a median follow-up of 9.5 months, median PFS in the 
      amivantamab-chemotherapy arm was 6.8 vs. 4.2 months in the chemotherapy arm 
      [hazard ratio (HR) 0.54, 95% confidence interval (CI): 0.37-0.81; nominal 
      P=0.002]. The ORR was 66% and 32%, respectively (odds ratio 4.0, 95% CI: 2.1-7.7; 
      nominal P<0.001) and median intracranial PFS was 12.5 and 8.5 months (HR 0.58, 
      95% CI: 0.34-1.00; nominal P=0.049). The safety profile in the Asian subgroup was 
      in line with the overall MARIPOSA-2 population. CONCLUSIONS: 
      Amivantamab-chemotherapy demonstrated superior PFS vs. chemotherapy in Asian 
      patients. Amivantamab-chemotherapy provides an effective option for patients 
      whose disease had progressed on third-generation EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitors.

TI  - Emerging landscape of antibody-drug conjugates in lung cancer: a clinical review.
LID - S2405-8033(26)00213-X [pii]
LID - 10.1016/j.trecan.2026.09.002 [doi]
AB  - Lung cancer is a heterogeneous disease, and while targeted therapies and 
      immunotherapies have improved patient outcomes, 5-year overall survival remains 
      poor at approximately 20%. Antibody-drug conjugates (ADCs) target tumors using a 
      targeted antibody that delivers a cytotoxic payload with clinically proven 
      antitumor activity, and over a dozen ADCs are under consideration for solid 
      tumors. Recent clinical trial data in metastatic non-small cell lung cancer have 
      led to FDA approval of three ADCs: trastuzumab deruxtecan for patients with human 
      epidermal growth factor receptor 2-mutated disease, telisotuzumab vedotin for 
      MET-overexpressing tumors, and datopotamab deruxtecan for patients with epidermal 
      growth factor receptor-mutated disease. In this article, we review the trajectory 
      of individual ADC targets in lung cancer, describe the latest clinical trial 
      results, and discuss future innovations in ADC therapies and potential mechanisms 
      of resistance.
CI  - Copyright (c) 2026 The Author(s). Published by Elsevier Inc. All rights reserved.

TI  - Inhibition of the MEK-ERK signaling pathway reverses acquired osimertinib 
      resistance in NSCLC attributed to AXL overexpression.
PG  - 124739
LID - S0024-3205(26)00548-5 [pii]
LID - 10.1016/j.lfs.2026.124739 [doi]
AB  - Osimertinib, a third-generation epidermal growth factor receptor tyrosine kinase 
      inhibitor (EGFR-TKI), is the standard first-line treatment for patients with 
      EGFR-activating mutation of late-stage non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC), but 
      the emergence of acquired resistance limits its efficacy in real use. The 
      overexpression of anexelekto (AXL) is known to be associated with acquired 
      resistance to osimertinib. However, the specific mechanisms of AXL-mediated 
      resistance are not well explained, nor of the specific therapeutic approaches in 
      patients for this. In this study, we identified that activation of MEK-ERK 
      pathway played a key role in AXL-driven acquired osimertinib resistance in NSCLC. 
      Inhibition of the MEK-ERK activation restored osimertinib sensitivity in 
      AXL-driven resistant tumor cells. The combination use of MEK inhibitor with 
      osimertinib reversed the aquierd osimertinib resistance in vitor and in vivo. Our 
      findings provide preclinical mechanistic support for potential use to targeting 
      MEK-ERK signaling in AXL-associated osimertinib resistance and forfurther 
      validation in patient-derived and clinical models.

PMID- 42868834
OWN - NLM

LR  - 20261013
IS  - 1618-095X (Electronic)

DP  - 2026 Oct 6
TI  - Xanthatin overcomes osimertinib resistance by disturbing cholesterol metabolism 
      via targeting HMGCR protein degradation in NSCLC.
PG  - 158857
LID - S0944-7113(26)01086-X [pii]
LID - 10.1016/j.phymed.2026.158857 [doi]
AB  - BACKGROUND: Osimertinib is a third-generation, irreversible EGFR tyrosine kinase 
      inhibitor (TKI), nevertheless, the inevitable occurrence of osimertinib 
      resistance after a long-term administration limits its clinical application in 
      non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC). Xanthatin, a naturally active component from 
      Xanthium Strumarium L., has been shown to possess multiple bioactive effects. 
      However, whether xanthatin can ameliorate osimertinib resistance remains unclear. 
      PURPOSE: The present study aimed to clarify the role and detailed mechanism of 
      xanthatin in reversing osimertinib resistance in NSCLC. METHODS: Combenefit 
      analysis, LDH release assay, and EdU staining were employed to evaluate the 
      effect of xanthatin on osimertinib cytotoxicity in osimertinib-resistant (OR) 
      NSCLC cells. Quantitative proteomics combined with bioinformatics and network 
      pharmacology analysis was used to uncover the specific target of xanthatin. 
      Xanthatin-triggered HMGCR protein degradation was assessed by bio-layer 
      interferometry, drug affinity responsive target stability assay, cycloheximide 
      chase assay and ubiquitination determination. The function of xanthatin on 
      cholesterol homeostasis was measured via the detection of HMGCR activity, total 
      cholesterol, triglycerides and lipid droplets as well as MDR1 distribution in the 
      lipid rafts. The function of MDR1 was examined via a rhodamine 123 efflux assay. 
      The accumulation and efflux of osimertinib were monitored using FITC-labeled 
      osimertinib. The effect of HMGCR on the cytotoxicity of xanthatin was evaluated 
      through a series of in vitro and in vivo experiments in HMGCR-overexpressing 
      NSCLC OR cells. RESULTS: Xanthatin restored the sensitivity of NSCLC OR cells to 
      osimertinib, accompanied by the induction of oxidative stress and mitochondrial 
      dysfunction. Proteomic data indicate that oxidative stress and metabolic 
      reprogramming contribute to xanthatin-induced apoptosis in NSCLC OR cells. 
      Through further integration with network pharmacology, we identified HMGCR (a key 
      enzyme of cholesterol metabolism) as a potential target of xanthatin. We 
      confirmed xanthatin directly binds to HMGCR protein (K(D)=6.753 muM), subsequently 
      facilitating AMFR-mediated K48-linked ubiquitination and degradation of HMGCR. 
      Xanthatin diminished intracellular cholesterol biosynthesis and MDR1 levels in 
      lipid rafts. HMGCR overexpression mitigated the cytotoxicity of xanthatin and 
      sustained cholesterol homeostasis, enabling tolerance to the combined effects of 
      xanthatin and osimertinib. Conversely, HMGCR-deficient NSCLC OR cells were more 
      vulnerable to osimertinib. CONCLUSION: Our study demonstrates for the first time 
      that xanthatin directly binds to HMGCR and promotes AMFR-mediated K48-linked 
      ubiquitination of HMGCR, which in turn accelerates HMGCR degradation through the 
      ubiquitin-proteasome pathway, leading to reduced cholesterol synthesis, less 
      distribution of MDR1 in lipid rafts, ROS accumulation, and mitochondrial 
      dysfunction-associated apoptosis, ultimately restoring osimertinib sensitivity in 
      NSCLC cells. Therefore, xanthatin may represent a preclinical natural product 
      candidate for the reversal of osimertinib resistance in NSCLC cells, which 
      requires further translational verification in future studies.
CI  - Copyright (c) 2026 Elsevier GmbH. All rights reserved.

TI  - Efficacy and safety of adjuvant ensartinib in stage I ALK-positive NSCLC 
      patients: a retrospective, multicenter study.
PG  - 109649
LID - S0169-5002(26)00710-5 [pii]
LID - 10.1016/j.lungcan.2026.109649 [doi]
AB  - BACKGROUND: The role of adjuvant ensartinib in patients with stage I ALK-positive 
      non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) with high-risk pathological features remains 
      unclear. This multicenter retrospective study aimed to evaluate the efficacy and 
      safety of adjuvant ensartinib in this population. METHODS: A total of 393 
      patients with completely resected stage I ALK-positive NSCLC were included from 
      five centers in China between March 2017 and March 2026. Patients were 
      categorized into the ensartinib group (n = 63) and the observation group 
      (n = 330). Inverse probability of treatment weighting (IPTW) and propensity score 
      matching (PSM) were employed to balance baseline characteristics. The primary 
      endpoint was disease-free survival (DFS). Secondary endpoints included overall 
      survival (OS) and safety. RESULTS: After IPTW, baseline characteristics were well 
      balanced between groups. The ensartinib group showed a higher 3-year DFS rate 
      compared with the observation group (100% vs. 90.2%;P = 0.014). In patients with 
      high-risk pathological features (micropapillary or solid subtypes or complex 
      glandular pathological subtypes >/=10%, mucinous adenocarcinoma, or spread through 
      air spaces), the 3-year DFS was 100% in the ensartinib group versus 86.5% in the 
      observation group (P = 0.016). No significant difference in OS was observed 
      between groups. Adverse events occurred in 87.3% of patients in the ensartinib 
      group, all of which were grade 1-2, with rash being the most common adverse event 
      (85.7%). CONCLUSION: Exploratory analyses suggested an association between 
      adjuvant ensartinib and favorable DFS outcomes in stage I ALK-positive NSCLC 
      patients exhibiting high-risk pathological features. A favorable safety profile 
      was also observed. For patients without high-risk factors, the benefit of 
      adjuvant ensartinib remains to be further clarified. These findings are 
      hypothesis-generating and support further prospective evaluation of risk-adapted 
      adjuvant ALK-TKI therapy in stage I ALK-positive NSCLC.
